It’s not just the city’s ancient heritage, UNESCO–listed monuments, or dramatic skyline that make it special—it’s the fact that it straddles two different continents. For travelers, that means twice as much to see and discover, though guided Istanbul tours make sightseeing on both the European and Asian sides accessible, not overwhelming. Half- and full-day Istanbul tours typically stop at blockbuster attractions including the Hagia Sophia, Blue Mosque, Grand Bazaar, and Topkapi Palace, while food tours cover everything from traditional markets to kebab shops. And it isn’t an Istanbul visit without a Bosphorus cruise: surely the most elegant way to continent-hop.\n\nRead more\n\nEnter dates\n\nTours\n\nFilters\n\nSort\n\n[All things to For those who paid the Basilica add-on and were not satisfied with the Serefiye option, a refund request was suggested. An important point to note is that in this area and generally throughout the city, there are very steep hills and sometimes stairs with many steps that could make it difficult for people with walking difficulties. We did not use public transportation due to unfamiliarity and did not find it very useful to buy the tourist card, therefore for long distances, we used taxis and preferably Uber at quite economical prices; as a recommendation, be especially careful when paying the taxi fare because even though they use the meter, on one occasion, the taxi driver kept money from our change, and also on some occasions, they left us a bit far from our destination because they had difficulty accessing the site due to traffic problems.\n\nThe food was delicious and reasonably priced, and it could be said economical, as long as we pay attention to the menu prices, since we experienced that at the Market we were at a restaurant with seafood, very good, but the cost of the fish was higher than what was listed on the menu. We also saw them both doing laps amongst the crowd to be sure everyone was happy and where they needed to be. Would recommend the morning tour early in your travels to Istanbul to get a feel for the area before venturing deeper into the city. From guided walks through ancient neighborhoods to breezy Bosphorus cruises with epic views, there’s a tour for every kind of traveler. And with so many options, knowing what to expect can help you squeeze the most magic out of your time here. Here’s the lowdown on Istanbul tours so you can pick the one that suits you.\n\n### Istanbul tours FAQ\n\n#### What are the best tours in Istanbul?\n\nIt depends on your vibe! Into history? Try a [walking tour](https://www.tripadvisor.com/Attractions-g293974-Activities-c42-t183-Istanbul.html) through the Old City. More into snacks? Food tours are where it’s at. Other fan favorites include [Bosphorus cruises](https://www.tripadvisor.com/Attractions-g293974-Activities-c55-Istanbul.html), shopping safaris in the [Grand Bazaar](https://www.tripadvisor.com/Attraction_Review-g293974-d294496-Reviews-Grand_Bazaar-Istanbul.html), and day trips to places like the [Princes' Islands](https://www.tripadvisor.com/Attraction_Review-g293974-d4288286-Reviews-Princes_Islands-Istanbul.html) if you’re feeling a little extra.\n\n#### When is the best time to go on an Istanbul tour?\n\nSpring (April to June) and fall (September to October) are your sweet spots. The weather’s just right—not too hot, not too chilly—and the crowds aren’t overwhelming. Bonus: Tulips pop off in spring, and early fall has those perfect warm days and breezy nights.\n\n#### Are there guided tours available for non-English speakers?\n\nYes! Plenty of tours are available in other languages like Spanish, French, German, and Italian. Just double-check with the provider ahead of time so you’re not relying on your three Duolingo phrases to get you through.\n\n#### What should I wear on a tour in Istanbul?\n\nComfy shoes and weather-suitable clothes are key—you’ll probably be on your feet a lot. If you’re heading into mosques or other religious spots, plan to cover your shoulders and knees. Ladies, it’s a good idea to bring a scarf for your head, just in case.\n\n#### What are the most popular attractions on Istanbul tours?\n\nYou’ve got the big hitters: Hagia Sophia, Blue Mosque, Topkapi Palace, Basilica Cistern, the Grand Bazaar, and scenic cruises along the Bosphorus. Some tours also mix in hidden gems like the Chora Church or the Spice Bazaar for that “I’ve seen more than just the main stuff” flex.\n\n#### Do I need to book Istanbul tours in advance?\n\nNot always—you can sometimes snag a spot the same day. But for popular tours or visits during the summer tourist rush, booking ahead is a super smart move. That way, you won’t miss out (or be stuck on a tour that doesn’t match up with your interests).\n\n#### Can I visit the Bosphorus on a tour?\n\nAbsolutely. Bosphorus cruises are a classic for a reason—you’ll sail past palaces, mosques, and mansions, with epic views in every direction. Options range from quick sightseeing spins to longer, fancier experiences with food and music.\n\n#### Are Istanbul tours wheelchair accessible?\n\nMany are, especially the newer or more modern ones. But heads up: Some historic sites have cobblestones, narrow steps, or limited access. Your best bet is to check directly with the tour company before booking to make sure the route works for your needs.\n\n#### What should I bring with me on a tour in Istanbul?\n\nThink comfort and sun protection: walking shoes, a water bottle, sunglasses, and maybe a hat. If you’re planning to visit religious sites, bring a scarf for modesty. And in cooler months, a light jacket never hurts—the weather can surprise you.\n\n#### How do I know if a tour in Istanbul is reputable?\n\nDo a little digging. Check reviews on Tripadvisor, look for licensed guides or official tour operators, and avoid anyone aggressively pitching you a “very special deal” on the street. If it sounds too good to be true… you know the rest.\n\n### Types of Istanbul tours\n\n#### Walking tours\n\nIf you're into stories, scenery, and getting your steps in, a [historical walking tour](https://www.tripadvisor.com/Attractions-g293974-Activities-c42-t228-Istanbul.html) is a solid pick. You'll cover the heavy hitters—Hagia Sophia, Blue Mosque, Topkapi Palace, Basilica Cistern, and the Hippodrome—while learning all about the city’s Byzantine bling and Ottoman grandeur. Perfect for first-timers, history buffs, or anyone who wants to casually drop “sultan” into conversation.\n\n#### Boat tours and cruises\n\nWant to see Istanbul from its best angle? Hop on a [Bosphorus cruise](https://www.tripadvisor.com/Attractions-g293974-Activities-c61-t167-Istanbul.html). You’ll glide past waterfront palaces, grand mosques, and mega-bridges with the city skyline doing its thing in the background. Go for a quick spin in the daytime, or level up with a dinner or sunset cruise—bonus points for dramatic selfies with seagulls.\n\n#### Food tours\n\nCome hungry, leave happy (and probably full of baklava). [Istanbul’s food tours](https://www.tripadvisor.com/Attractions-g293974-Activities-c42-t204-Istanbul.html) take you deep into the city’s legendary culinary scene covering street eats, market bites, and restaurant gems. Expect everything from juicy kebabs and crispy börek to meze spreads and Turkish delight that lives up to the name.\n\n#### Shopping and bazaar tours\n\nLost in the Grand Bazaar? You’re not alone. [Shopping tours](https://www.tripadvisor.com/Attractions-g293974-Activities-c42-t242-Istanbul.html) are your secret weapon for navigating the chaos. A good guide will show you where to find the best spices, carpets, ceramics, and gold— _and_ how to haggle like a pro.\n\n#### Cultural and art tours\n\nIstanbul isn’t just history—it’s also buzzing with modern culture and creativity. [Cultural tours](https://www.tripadvisor.com/Attractions-g293974-Activities-c42-t225-Istanbul.html) might take you to contemporary art galleries, cool museums like Istanbul Modern, or workshops where you can try your hand at Turkish calligraphy or traditional crafts. Great for channeling your inner artist (or just impressing your Instagram followers).\n\n#### Day trips\n\nNeed a break from the city’s hustle? [Day trips](https://www.tripadvisor.com/Attractions-g293974-Activities-c63-Istanbul.html) are your escape plan. Sail off to the car-free charm of the Princes' Islands, or take a scenic journey to places like Gallipoli, Troy, or Bursa. Whether you're after beach vibes, history, or both—there's a day trip for that.\n\n#### Private tours\n\nIf you’re after a tour that fits _your_ interests, not someone else’s checklist, [private tours](https://www.tripadvisor.com/Attractions-g293974-Activities-c42-t235-Istanbul.html) are the way to go. Whether you’re into architecture, photography, food, or finding Istanbul’s best-kept secrets, a private guide will tailor the day to you. It’s the VIP way to explore—great for couples, families, or anyone who prefers their sightseeing with a personal touch.\n\n### Tour departure locations\n\nMost tours depart from central locations that are easily accessible for tourists, including:\n\n#### Sultanahmet\n\nThis is the historic heart of the city—and the unofficial HQ for walking tours. If you're near the Hagia Sophia, Blue Mosque, or Topkapi Palace, congrats! You're basically already at the starting line. Most history-heavy tours kick off here because, well, it's where all the history is.\n\n#### Taksim Square\n\nModern Istanbul’s main stage. Taksim is a go-to departure spot for tours that involve a bus, a boat, or a little bit of both. It’s also near Istiklal Street—aka shopping central—so it’s perfect if you want to sneak in a little retail therapy before or after your adventure.\n\n#### Eminönü\n\nIf you're hopping on a Bosphorus cruise, chances are you’ll start in Eminönü. Located right on the Golden Horn, it’s a bustling (and super photogenic) launch point with easy access to docks, ferries, fish sandwiches, and seagulls that like to get after any stray sandwich crumbs.\n\n#### Kabataş\n\nAnother hot spot for boat tours, Kabataş is a transport hub with ferries galore, including the ones that connect the European and Asian sides. Many Bosphorus cruises and day trips to the Princes’ Islands set sail from here, so keep your sea legs ready.\n\n#### Hotels\n\nDon’t feel like navigating the city to a meeting point? No worries. Lots of private or multi-stop tours offer hotel pick-up—especially if you’re staying in Sultanahmet, Taksim, or Galata. Just check ahead to see if your slippers-to-sightseeing dreams can come true.\n\nRead more\n\nIstanbul\n\n[Istanbul Tourism](https://www.tripadvisor.com/Tourism-g293974-Istanbul-Vacations.html)
